        ### Task Summary
        Sprint 1 of the Astrid reshape introduces per-tab sessions, a `runs/<ulid>/lease.json` writer-epoch, and — most critically — a flock-protected event-append helper with last-hash + epoch CAS that replaces the current unsafe `append_event` in `astrid/core/task/events.py`. All mutating verbs and inbox consumption must route through that locked path. Sprint 1 also adds `~/.astrid` identity/config/sessions plumbing, a new `astrid attach / sessions / status` CLI surface with a session gate on every other verb, deletes `<project>/active_run.json` and the `astrid threads` subsystem (migrating both into session bindings), and writes a `default_timeline_id: null` sentinel into `project.json` for Sprint 2.

        ### Key Evidence
        - Current `append_event` (astrid/core/task/events.py:25) does verify_chain → read_events → compute prev_hash → open("a") → write. There is NO `flock` anywhere — this is the multi-tab race the sprint is fixing. The new helper must do flock(LOCK_EX) → re-read last line and parse hash → CAS against writer's expected_prev_hash → re-read lease.json's writer_epoch → CAS against expected_writer_epoch → append → fsync → release lock. (source: astrid/core/task/events.py:25-50; relevance: high)

        ### Constraints
        - Sprint 1 must NOT change the step model. No edits to plan.py / inbox.py (beyond switching to the locked append helper and dropping clear_active_run) / step adapter logic.
- Sprint 1 must NOT implement the timeline container — only write a `default_timeline_id: null` sentinel into project.json.
- Sprint 1 must NOT touch RunPod / vibecomfy / hype port / auditing verbs (`run show`, `artifacts`, `trace`) — those are Sprints 5a / 5b.
- The locked-append helper must hold ONE flock(LOCK_EX) across last-hash re-read + epoch re-read + append + fsync. Splitting these is the bug.
- Both CAS failures (stale tail, stale epoch) must raise typed exceptions including the conflicting values in the message — no silent fallbacks anywhere in the lease/append path.
- Takeover must atomically increment writer_epoch in the SAME locked write that swaps attached_session.json. The takeover event is observability; the lock + CAS is the fence.
- All existing Sprint 0 tests must continue to pass (env-inheritance spike, flock-APFS spike, two-tab harness smoke).
- Migration scripts default to --dry-run; --apply commits; idempotent; log per-action to stderr; per-project audit log to temp file. Any data loss in `active_run.json` migration is a STOP-LINE.
- Migration script that adds `default_timeline_id` must keep `project.json` valid against the existing validator (decide additive vs schema-version-bump).
- Astrid is now a SESSION-FIRST CLI: only attach, status, projects ls, projects create, sessions ls, sessions takeover, init, --help are allowed unbound. Every other verb errors with a clear hint.
- Subprocesses must inherit ASTRID_SESSION_ID — confirmed by Sprint 0 env-inheritance spike; do not silently scrub env in any new code path.
- SKILL.md and AGENTS.md are symlinks to astrid/packs/_core/skill/SKILL.md — edit the target. Stop-hook preamble in lifecycle.py:_AGENT_MD_TEMPLATE + PROHIBITION_PREAMBLE must be updated to re-inject the new status-first instruction.
- Branch must be `reshape/sprint-1` off `reshape/sprint-0`.
- Type hints everywhere; Pathlib over os.path; no comments unless the WHY is non-obvious.

        ### Suggested Approach
        Build bottom-up so each layer is independently testable before integrating into the CLI:

1. **Branch + scaffold.** `git checkout -b reshape/sprint-1 reshape/sprint-0`. Create `astrid/core/session/` package with empty modules: paths.py, model.py, binding.py, lease.py, identity.py, config.py, discovery.py.

2. **Lease + locked-append (the apex).** First, define `class StaleTailError(EventLogError)` and `class StaleEpochError(EventLogError)` in events.py. Then write `append_event_locked(run_dir: Path, event: dict, *, expected_writer_epoch: int, expected_prev_hash: str) -> dict` that:
   - opens `run_dir / 'events.jsonl'` in append mode
   - `fcntl.flock(fh.fileno(), fcntl.LOCK_EX)` (blocking)
   - reads the current tail line; parses its hash; if `last_hash != expected_prev_hash` raise StaleTailError including both
   - reads `run_dir / 'lease.json'`'s writer_epoch; if `current != expected_writer_epoch` raise StaleEpochError
   - computes new hash from `last_hash`, writes the JSON line + newline, flushes, `os.fsync`, releases lock
   - returns the stored dict
   Keep the existing `append_event` as a thin wrapper that reads epoch + prev_hash internally — that lets legacy single-writer paths keep working without the CAS, but they still get serialized by flock so they never interleave.
   Unit-test in `tests/task/test_events_locked.py` (happy path, stale tail, stale epoch, succeeds after epoch bump).
   Then run the flock-spike pattern but invoke the new helper from two subprocesses and assert exactly-one-winner.

3. **Session model + paths.** In `session/model.py` define `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Session(id, project, timeline, run_id, agent_id, attached_at, last_used_at, role)` with `from_path/to_path` JSON helpers. In `session/paths.py` mirror `core/project/paths.py` patterns: env-var override `ASTRID_HOME` (default `~/.astrid`), helpers for `sessions_dir()`, `identity_path()`, `user_config_path()`, `workspace_config_path()`. ULID: vendor a ~20-line generator (or pull `python-ulid`) — record the choice in a one-line comment. Round-trip tests for all readers/writers.

4. **Lease.** `session/lease.py`: `read_lease(run_dir)`, `write_lease_init(run_dir, session_id, writer_epoch=0)`, `bump_epoch_and_swap_session(run_dir, new_session_id) -> int` which acquires the SAME flock on events.jsonl, increments writer_epoch, swaps attached_session.json, appends a takeover event, releases. Reuse `append_event_locked` so the takeover event itself is properly chained.

5. **Binding.** `session/binding.py::resolve_current() -> Session | None` reads `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` env, looks up the session file. `is_writer_for(session, run_dir) -> bool` compares lease.attached_session_id.

6. **Identity + config + discovery.** Identity reader/writer + first-run bootstrap (prompts for agent_id slug). Config reader merges `~/.astrid/config.json` + `.astrid/config.json` (workspace wins). Discovery enumerates projects under `resolve_projects_root()`.

7. **Verb implementations.** Add a new module `astrid/core/session/cli.py` with `cmd_attach`, `cmd_sessions_ls`, `cmd_sessions_detach`, `cmd_sessions_takeover`. Rewrite `cmd_status` in lifecycle.py (or move it) to be session-driven and print the full breadcrumb. cmd_status with no identity → bootstrap; with identity but no session → list projects + attach hint; bound → breadcrumb.

8. **CLI gate.** In `astrid/pipeline.py:main`, after the existing nudge and before `_dispatch`, add a gate: if first token is NOT in the allowlist `{attach, sessions, status, projects, init, -h, --help}` (and projects' sub-verb must be ls/create when unbound), require ASTRID_SESSION_ID to resolve a valid session, else print the error + recovery. Wire `attach`, `sessions` dispatch entries. Remove the `thread` dispatch entry.

9. **Migrate callers off active_run.py.** Sweep the 4+2 sites in lifecycle.py, gate.py:519, inbox.py:216, hook.py:40/66, orchestrate/test_runner.py:113. Replace with: resolve session → load run_dir → use lease for CAS. Route every `append_event(events_path, ev)` call through `append_event_locked(run_dir, ev, expected_writer_epoch=epoch, expected_prev_hash=prev)` where `epoch` is read at the start of the verb and `prev_hash` is read just before append. On StaleEpochError/StaleTailError, the verb exits non-zero with a clean message — the agent retries.

10. **Threads removal.** Delete `astrid/threads/`, the pipeline.py:123-126 dispatch entry, the 13 test_threads_*.py files. Update SKILL.md (the symlink target) to remove the line-60 instruction and replace with the new attach/status workflow. Run the SKILL inspect test to see what assumes threads — likely test_threads_docs_skill_inspect.py is deleted along with the rest.

11. **project.json sentinel.** Add `default_timeline_id` as optional in `build_project`. Update `validate_project` to preserve it (it already preserves unknown fields, but make it explicit + typed).

12. **Migration scripts under `scripts/migrations/sprint-1/`:**
    - `migrate_active_run_to_sessions.py` — for each `<project>/active_run.json`, materialize one session binding (using the project root's name as slug, ULID-generated session id) and delete the file.
    - `migrate_threads_to_sessions.py` — read `.astrid/threads.json` (and any per-project thread state), fold any persistent fields (e.g., variant selections) into session bindings or, if irrelevant after threads die, log + skip. Confirm with the user that no run-history field is being dropped.
    - `migrate_set_default_timeline_sentinel.py` — for each `project.json`, ensure `default_timeline_id` key exists with `null`.
    All three: `#!/usr/bin/env python3`, executable, `--dry-run` default, `--apply` flag, stderr per-action log, temp-file audit log per project.

13. **SKILL.md / AGENTS.md rewrite.** Step 1: `astrid status` always. Document `attach`, `next`, `ack`, `sessions ls/detach/takeover`. Update PROHIBITION_PREAMBLE and `_AGENT_MD_TEMPLATE` in lifecycle.py so the stop-hook re-injection teaches the new model.

14. **Tests + harness validation.** Add the listed test files. Run the two-tab harness 100 rounds on a real mutating verb (pick `astrid ack <step> --decision approve` because it has the cleanest single-event-append surface, or even a thin `astrid sessions takeover` test that exercises lease swap). Confirm exactly-one-winner. Then run the takeover-during-append test under multiple seeds.

15. **Regression sweep.** Run all of `pytest tests/` (excluding deleted thread tests). Fix any failure that turns out to be a real escape rather than test rot. Confirm Sprint 0 spike + harness tests still green. Run any hype-related smoke test under the new model and confirm functional non-regression (no byte-equivalence required per regression-workload.md).

16. **Stop-line checks before merge.**
    - Two-tab harness: 100 rounds, exactly one winner per round, no interleaving in events.jsonl.
    - Tab restart + ASTRID_SESSION_ID re-export restores binding.
    - active_run migration: dry-run shows expected actions; --apply produces session bindings + deletes file; events.jsonl unchanged; no run-history loss.

        The engineering brief above was produced by analyzing the codebase. Use it as a strong starting point, but the suggested approach is a hypothesis — verify it's the best fix, not just a valid one.

        Idea:
# Sprint 1 — Sessions and the binding contract

This is Sprint 1 of the Astrid reshape (full plan: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid/idea.md` — Sprint 1 section is canonical for scope; this brief expands it for execution).

Working directory: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid`. Branch: create `reshape/sprint-1` off `reshape/sprint-0` and work there.

## Goal

End multi-tab races. Make "what am I bound to" a first-class, queryable thing. Ship `astrid status` as the agent's mandated anchor verb. Lay down the lease + locked-append contract every later sprint depends on.

**No timelines yet (Sprint 2). No new step model yet (Sprint 3).** Sprint 1 is purely the session/binding/safety layer.

## Foundational invariant (the apex contract)

The single most important deliverable is the **locked event-append path**, because every later sprint runs on top of it. The contract is:

> Every event appended to `runs/<ulid>/events.jsonl` does this **atomically inside one critical section** held by `flock(events.jsonl, LOCK_EX)`:
> 1. Re-read the file's last line, parse its hash, compare against the writer's read-time hash. If they differ → reject with a stale-tail error.
> 2. Re-read `runs/<ulid>/lease.json`'s `writer_epoch`. Compare against the writer's read-time epoch. If they differ → reject with a stale-epoch error.
> 3. Compute the new event's previous-hash = the last-line hash.
> 4. Append the new event line.
> 5. fsync (or equivalent durability), then release the lock.

A stale writer that passed its initial epoch check but lost the race to a takeover **gets rejected at append time, not silently committed**. The `flock` + last-hash CAS + epoch CAS are the actual fence; the takeover *event* is just observability.

This replaces the current `append_event` in `astrid/core/task/events.py`, which does read/verify/append **without a file lock** (the bug we're fixing).

The Sprint 0 flock-on-APFS spike confirmed `flock` is reliable for this workload. Use the worked test pattern from `tests/spikes/test_flock_apfs.py` as the integration-test starting point.

## Decisions (locked; do not relitigate)

- **Session id = ULID.** Stored in `~/.astrid/sessions/<ulid>.json` with `{session_id, project, timeline, run_id, agent_id, attached_at, last_used_at, role}`. `role` ∈ `writer | reader`.
- **`ASTRID_SESSION_ID` env var binds the tab.** Subprocesses inherit through `fork`/`exec`. Sprint 0 verified all existing subprocess paths preserve env via `{**os.environ, ...}`.
- **Stable agent identity in `~/.astrid/identity.json`** with `agent_id: <slug>` (e.g. `claude-1`, `codex-research`). First-run bootstrap prompts user to set one. Sessions inherit it; per-tab override via `astrid attach --as agent:<id>`.
- **Per-user default project in `~/.astrid/config.json`; per-workspace override in `.astrid/config.json`.** Neither auto-attaches — both feed the suggestion shown by `status` when unbound.
- **Per-project default timeline pointer recorded in `project.json`** (Sprint 1 writes the field; Sprint 2 wires it into `astrid attach`). Use a sentinel value in S1's migration; Sprint 2 backfills.
- **Read-only attach when a run is held by another session.** Second attacher gets `role: reader`. Takeover is the explicit verb `astrid sessions takeover <session_id>`.
- **Run lease epoch (`writer_epoch: int`).** Stored in `runs/<ulid>/lease.json` alongside `attached_session.json` (the current writer's session id). Every mutating verb routes through the locked-append helper above.
- **Takeover atomically increments `writer_epoch`** in the same locked write that swaps `attached_session.json`. Both old session and new session see a `takeover` event in `events.jsonl` with `{prev_session, new_session, prev_epoch, new_epoch, at}`.
- **Stuck-attachment recovery via `status`.** A fresh tab's `status` proactively flags suspected-dead sessions (criterion: no event written in N seconds AND session-file mtime older than M seconds — pick honest defaults like 60s/300s) and offers takeover with a "may still be live elsewhere — confirm" warning. Manual `astrid sessions detach <id>` is the unconditional escape hatch.
- **Threads die.** `thread show @active` and friends are removed. Any persistent thread state migrates into session bindings in a one-shot pass.
- **Inbox primitive standardized.** Every run has `runs/<ulid>/inbox/`. Sessions and external systems write JSON files; `astrid next` consumes them. This is the substrate that makes async work possible from day one.
- **First-run bootstrap is part of this sprint, not assumed.** Fresh tab post-migration must not produce a string of errors. When `~/.astrid/identity.json` is missing OR no default project is set, `astrid` prompts through identity + project discovery + default selection explicitly. `astrid status` when unbound + no default lists discoverable projects with the `attach` command spelled out (not just "no session").
- **Migration sets a per-project default-timeline sentinel** even though Sprint 2 wires the actual timeline container. Otherwise Sprint 2 has to backfill across the entire project tree.
- **CLI gate.** Every verb except `attach`, `status`, `projects ls`, `projects create`, `sessions ls`, `sessions takeover`, `init` (first-run bootstrap), and `--help` errors out with a clear "no session bound" message that suggests `astrid attach <project>`.

## Deliverables

### New / rewritten files

- **`astrid/core/session/`** new package:
  - `paths.py` — helpers for `~/.astrid/sessions/<ulid>.json`, `~/.astrid/identity.json`, `~/.astrid/config.json`, `.astrid/config.json` (workspace).
  - `model.py` — `Session` dataclass (id, project, timeline, run_id, agent_id, attached_at, last_used_at, role) with json read/write.
  - `binding.py` — resolve current session via `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` env var; readers and writers; "is this session a writer for this run" checks.
  - `lease.py` — `runs/<ulid>/lease.json` reader/writer; `attached_session.json` reader/writer.
  - `identity.py` — `~/.astrid/identity.json` reader/writer + first-run bootstrap.
  - `config.py` — config file reader (user + workspace) + default project/timeline resolution.
  - `discovery.py` — list discoverable projects under the projects root for the bootstrap UX.

- **`astrid/core/task/events.py`** rewrite the append path:
  - New `append_event_locked(run_dir, event_dict, *, expected_writer_epoch, expected_prev_hash) -> EventRecord` that does flock + last-hash CAS + epoch CAS atomically. Raises `StaleTailError` / `StaleEpochError` on conflict.
  - Old `append_event` becomes a thin wrapper over the locked path that reads epoch + last-hash internally; deprecate it for callers that need the CAS guarantees.
  - All existing call sites that mutate the event log MUST route through the new locked helper.

- **`astrid/core/task/active_run.py`** delete or stub:
  - The `<project>/active_run.json` file is the multi-tab race we're fixing. Migrate any existing files into per-tab session bindings (one-shot at `astrid attach`), then remove the read/write helpers. Leave a one-line stub explaining what replaced it.

- **Threads subsystem removal:**
  - Find every `thread show @active`-style verb and any `astrid/core/thread/` (or wherever threads live). Migrate persistent thread state into session bindings (best-effort one-shot migration script). Then remove the subsystem.

### New / rewritten verbs

Use the existing CLI module (`astrid/cli/` or wherever verbs are registered — discover by reading current code).

- `astrid attach <project> [--timeline <slug>] [--session <id>] [--as agent:<id>]` — bind the current tab to a project. Creates a new session (writes `~/.astrid/sessions/<ulid>.json`), prints the `ASTRID_SESSION_ID=<ulid>` line for the user to `export`. If `--session <id>` given, resume an existing session. If a run is held by another writer, attach as reader and tell the user how to take over.
- `astrid sessions ls` — list all sessions in `~/.astrid/sessions/` with their bound project + timeline + run + last-used.
- `astrid sessions detach [<id>]` — explicit detach. With no id, detaches the current tab.
- `astrid sessions takeover <run-id|session-id>` — atomic epoch-bump + lease swap. Emits a `takeover` event into the run's `events.jsonl`. Refuses if the session being taken over wrote within the last N seconds without confirmation flag.
- `astrid status` — completely rewritten. When bound, prints the full breadcrumb: `session <id> · agent <id> · project <slug> · timeline <slug> · run <id> · current step <name> · recent events (last 5) · inbox count · role (writer/reader) · takeover hint when reader`. When unbound: lists discoverable projects, suggests defaults if set, spells out the exact `astrid attach <slug>` command. When `~/.astrid/identity.json` missing: triggers the first-run bootstrap.
- **CLI gate** on every other verb: read `ASTRID_SESSION_ID`, look up the session file, error-out with a clear hint if either is missing.

### Migration scripts

Place under `scripts/migrations/sprint-1/`:

- `migrate_active_run_to_sessions.py` — for each project with an `active_run.json`, materialize a session binding for "the calling tab" (best-effort: one session per project), then delete the file. Idempotent. Dry-run by default; `--apply` flag to commit.
- `migrate_threads_to_sessions.py` — same shape for thread state, if it exists in the codebase.
- `migrate_set_default_timeline_sentinel.py` — for each project, write a sentinel `default_timeline_id: null` field into `project.json` so Sprint 2 doesn't have to backfill.

Each script:
- Logs every action to stderr.
- Supports `--dry-run` (default) and `--apply`.
- Writes a per-project audit log to a temp file.

### SKILL.md / AGENTS.md rewrite

Both files are stale (predate the task framework, don't teach `astrid status` / `next` / `ack`). Rewrite around the new mandated workflow:

1. **First instruction**: "Run `astrid status` before any other verb. If unbound, ask the user which project."
2. Document `attach`, `status`, `next`, `ack`, `sessions ls/detach/takeover`.
3. Stop-hook preamble (if Astrid has one — discover by reading current SKILL.md) is updated to re-inject the status-first instruction when context decays.

### Tests

- **Locked-append unit tests** (`tests/task/test_events_locked.py`): single-process correctness — append, append-after-stale-tail rejected, append-after-stale-epoch rejected, append after epoch bump succeeds.
- **Two-tab integration tests** (use the existing `tests/concurrency/two_tab_harness.py` from Sprint 0): two writers race the same run; exactly one succeeds, the other gets stale-tail or stale-epoch. Run with `--count 100` to surface races.
- **Takeover atomicity test**: takeover-during-append must either (a) the append wins and takeover sees stale tail and retries, or (b) the takeover wins and the appender sees stale epoch and rejects. Never both succeed; never silent loss.
- **Session attach/detach/resume tests** (unit + small integration).
- **Migration script tests**: each script has a smoke test with a tiny fixture project tree.
- **First-run bootstrap test**: simulate missing `~/.astrid/identity.json` → bootstrap prompt path runs end-to-end (mock the prompt input).
- **CLI gate test**: every gated verb errors clearly when `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` is unset.

## Stop-lines (halt and rethink, do not push through)

1. **If the locked-append path can't be proven correct under contention** (the two-tab harness shows interleaved writes or silent epoch-violations under any seed), halt. The whole reshape is built on this guarantee.
2. **If session resume across tab restart is broken** (closing a tab + reopening + `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` re-export doesn't restore the binding), halt. The "sessions are resumable, never auto-expire" decision is load-bearing.
3. **If migration of `active_run.json` causes ANY data loss** (any project's run-history becomes unreadable), halt and restore from the Sprint 0 snapshot.

## Out of scope (Sprint 2 / 3 territory — do NOT touch)

- Timeline as container (Sprint 2). The `default_timeline_id` field is a sentinel only; do not implement the timeline schema.
- Step model rewrite (Sprint 3). `plan.py` / `inbox.py` / step adapter logic stays as-is; only the event-append path changes.
- Plan mutation verbs (Sprint 3).
- Any RunPod / vibecomfy work.
- Hype port.
- Auditing verbs (`astrid run show / artifacts / trace`) — those are Sprint 5a.

## Acceptance criteria

- `git branch --show-current` returns `reshape/sprint-1`.
- All Sprint 0 tests still pass (regression).
- `pytest tests/task/test_events_locked.py tests/concurrency/ tests/session/ -v` all green.
- Two-tab harness: race the same `astrid attach` + locked-append-via-some-verb 100 times; exactly one writer succeeds per race, the other gets a clean stale-tail or stale-epoch error.
- Fresh tab + no identity file → `astrid status` triggers first-run bootstrap, completes successfully, leaves `~/.astrid/identity.json` written.
- Fresh tab + identity but no session bound → `astrid status` lists discoverable projects + spells out `astrid attach <slug>`.
- Fresh tab + session bound → `astrid status` prints the full breadcrumb.
- `astrid sessions takeover` of a stale-looking session works; the old session's next mutating verb gets rejected with stale-epoch.
- All migration scripts run cleanly against the Sprint 0 baseline inventory (refer to `docs/reshape/inventory-baseline-20260511.csv`).
- The Sprint 0 pinned regression workload (`docs/reshape/regression-workload.md`) re-runs cleanly. **Note:** that doc reports no past hype runs exist on this machine, so the regression gate is "no functional regression" rather than "byte-equivalent output." Run any existing hype-related smoke tests and confirm green.
- SKILL.md / AGENTS.md updated; a fresh-context agent reading them can complete one full task end-to-end without asking a clarifying question.

## Useful repo references

- `astrid/core/task/events.py` — the unsafe `append_event` to replace.
- `astrid/core/task/active_run.py` — the file to migrate away from.
- `astrid/core/project/paths.py` — project / run path helpers; reuse the patterns.
- `tests/spikes/test_flock_apfs.py` — Sprint 0's flock spike; the worked pattern for the locked-append integration tests.
- `tests/concurrency/two_tab_harness.py` — Sprint 0's race harness.
- `docs/reshape/spike-env-inheritance.md` — confirms `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` survives every subprocess path.
- `docs/reshape/spike-flock-apfs.md` — confirms `flock` is reliable for this workload.
- `idea.md` § Foundation > Load-bearing decisions — apex spec.
- `idea.md` § Sprint 1 — the canonical sprint definition this brief expands.

## Style notes

- Type hints everywhere. Pathlib over os.path.
- New tests follow existing test naming + structure conventions in the repo.
- Migration scripts: shebang, `chmod +x`, `--dry-run` is the default.
- No silent fallbacks in the lease/append path — every CAS failure raises a typed exception with the conflicting values in the message.

Top-of-file comment documents lease-first write ordering contract: 'Producers (cmd_start) MUST write runs/<id>/lease.json first, then <project>/current_run.json. Readers (WriterContext auto-rebind) read current_run.json first and rely on the lease being present.' Add round-trip tests at `tests/session/test_lease.py` (incl. plan_hash + atomic swap + concurrent takeover) and `tests/test_current_run.py`.",

      "executor_notes": "Verified: ulid.py is a thin re-export from astrid.threads.ids per DEC-001. paths.py honors ASTRID_HOME env (default ~/.astrid). model.py exposes the frozen Session dataclass with role: Literal['writer','reader','orphan-pending'] and Session.from_json/to_json. lease.py implements the documented schema {writer_epoch, attached_session_id, plan_hash}; bump_epoch_and_swap_session writes the new lease (epoch N+1) first under fcntl.flock(LOCK_EX) on events.jsonl, then computes+writes the takeover event INLINE under the same already-held flock (post-bump epoch = N+1 is the value the locked-append contract sees) \u2014 re-entering append_event_locked would deadlock on the same fd. claim_orphan_lease also bumps epoch by 1 and refuses warm leases; release_writer_lease preserves epoch + plan_hash. astrid/core/project/current_run.py documents the lease-first write ordering contract in its module docstring. 13 tests (9 lease + 4 current_run) all pass, including a spawn-subprocess concurrent-takeover test that confirms two serialized bumps land cleanly at epoch=2 with chain verifying.",

      "id": "T6",
      "description": "Implement WriterContext in `astrid/core/session/writer.py` with auto-rebind AND writer-authorization check. Define `NoRunBoundError(Exception)` LOCALLY in this module (NOT in events.py \u2014 it is a session-state condition, not an event-log error). __enter__: (1) read current_run.json; if on_disk_run_id != self.session.run_id, replace session via dataclasses.replace(self.session, run_id=on_disk_run_id, last_used_at=now()) AND rewrite session file via self.session.to_json(session_path(...)); (2) if session.run_id is None raise NoRunBoundError; (3) set self.run_dir = project_dir(self.session.project)/'runs'/self.session.run_id; (4) read lease; (5) WRITER-AUTH CHECK: if lease['attached_session_id'] != self.session.id raise NotWriterError(session_id=self.session.id, writer_id=lease['attached_session_id']); (6) capture self.expected_writer_epoch=lease['writer_epoch'], self.plan_hash=lease['plan_hash']. Provide an append(event) helper that calls append_event_locked with captured epoch and freshly-read tail (under flock). Provide `writer_context_from_decision(decision: GateDecision) -> WriterContext` factory that also performs the writer-auth check on entry. Docstring MUST explicitly note: 'On entry, WriterContext may rewrite the session file on disk if run_id changed via auto-rebind. Tests that snapshot the session file before invoking a verb must re-read after via the attached_session fixture's refresh() helper.' Tests at `tests/session/test_writer_context.py`: happy path, stale-epoch surfacing, stale-tail surfacing, test_reader_session_cannot_write (reader \u2192 NotWriterError, events.jsonl unchanged), test_orphan_pending_cannot_write (lease attached_session_id=None \u2192 NotWriterError, then takeover promotes), auto-rebind across run_id change.",

    "cmd_start write ordering: lease.json FIRST (atomic tmp+os.replace), THEN current_run.json (atomic tmp+os.replace). Inline code comment. Any reader observing the new current_run.json must be guaranteed to find a corresponding lease.json.",

  "meta_commentary": "Sprint 1 is the load-bearing safety/binding layer that the rest of the Astrid reshape depends on. Build BOTTOM-UP: the locked event-append helper (T2) must be proven correct under contention (T3 \u2014 STOP-LINE at 100 rounds) BEFORE anything else; halt if interleaving or silent epoch violations surface. Six concrete v4 design fixes to internalize: (1) DROP tail-only chain re-verification \u2014 events don't store prev_hash; StaleTailError CAS is sufficient (FLAG-019). (2) WriterContext.__enter__ MUST writer-auth-check `lease['attached_session_id'] == session.id` and raise NotWriterError otherwise. (3) cmd_start writes lease.json FIRST, then current_run.json (lease-first ordering prevents torn-write window for auto-rebind readers). (4) SD-023 amendment: 'no session bound' is the documented exception to preamble-precedes-every-message. (5) Migration aborts hard on malformed active_run.json by default; --force-skip-malformed for opt-in; --dry-run previews without aborting. (6) WriterContext auto-rebind silently rewrites the session file \u2014 document side effect, give fixtures a refresh() helper. Open executor-level polish items from the gate warnings: NoRunBoundError lives in writer.py (not events.py); bump_epoch_and_swap_session passes expected_writer_epoch=N+1 (post-bump) to its internal append; gate_command's writer_epoch kwarg is dead under WriterContext-at-entry \u2014 remove it. astrid/threads/ STAYS (DEC-001) \u2014 only the user-facing CLI verb is removed; hype regression depends on it. Reuse astrid.core.project.jsonio.write_json_atomic for atomic writes (recurring debt). T16 is the final validation gate combining all STOP-LINEs; do not declare done until the post-race verify_chain smoke confirms mid-chain integrity is preserved despite tail-only CAS in the hot path.",

        "criterion": "`astrid attach <project>` mints a ULID and writes session file. Three role outcomes: writer (no current_run.json), reader (current_run.json + lease.attached_session_id=other), orphan-pending (current_run.json + lease.attached_session_id=null) with takeover hint",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Session resume across tab restart works \u2014 STOP-LINE",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`astrid sessions takeover` handles both cases atomically: bump_epoch_and_swap_session for live lease; claim_orphan_lease for unclaimed lease (sets attached_session_id AND bumps epoch). Both emit takeover events",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests",
          "read_files"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "First-run bootstrap: identity.json absent \u2192 `astrid status` runs bootstrap, writes identity.json, exits cleanly",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`astrid status` bound matches literal breadcrumb template (incl. role: writer/reader/orphan-pending)",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "CLI gate: non-allowlisted verbs error clearly when ASTRID_SESSION_ID unset; allowlist is exactly {attach, status, projects ls/create, sessions ls/takeover/detach, init, -h, --help} plus `author test --project` as developer/CI exception; doctor and setup require a session",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`cmd_next` session check runs BEFORE preamble print; SD-023 is amended in preamble.py top-of-file comment and at lifecycle.py:407-411 to document that 'no session bound' precedes preamble (the explicit exception to the invariant)",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "read_files",
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Verb functions accept `session: Session` argument; tests use `attached_session` fixture; contract documented in astrid/core/session/__init__.py",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "read_files"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Stop-hook: session-bound resolution takes precedence; cwd-walk fallback only; projects-root scan removed",
        "priority": "must",

          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "`gate_command(slug, command, evidence, *, root, writer_epoch=None)` accepts writer_epoch kwarg; consume_inbox_entry threads its outer WriterContext's epoch through. Takeover-during-inbox-consume legitimately surfaces StaleEpochError",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "read_files",
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Each project.json gains `default_timeline_id: null` or valid slug after migration; validate_project explicitly type-checks",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "All three migration scripts default to --dry-run, support --apply, log to stderr, write audit logs, idempotent. active-run migration writes lease.json with migrated plan_hash",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "read_files",
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Migration script aborts hard on malformed active_run.json (invalid JSON, non-sha256:hex plan_hash, missing run_id) with STOP-LINE message by default; `--force-skip-malformed` flag opts in to skip-with-audit. Test `test_migrate_active_run_malformed_aborts` covers both paths",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "read_files",
          "run_tests"
        ]
      },
      {
        "criterion": "Active-run \u2192 current_run + lease migration: events.jsonl bytes unchanged; current_run.json written with run_id; lease.json written with migrated plan_hash; active_run.json removed \u2014 STOP-LINE",
        "priority": "must",
        "requires": [
          "run_tests"
        ]

        "priority": "must",

        "priority": "should",

        "priority": "should",

Does the fix handle all of them?: gate_command writer_epoch kwarg threading (callers-2): plan adds `writer_epoch: int | None = None`. inbox.consume_inbox_entry captures the outer WriterContext's epoch and passes it through. Subtle: the inbox-driven re-entry happens from inside cmd_next's WriterContext, but consume_inbox_entry calls gate_command at inbox.py:259 which then needs to APPEND events via the same lock. Since the outer WriterContext's lock is released between appends (each append re-acquires), there's no actual lock-nesting issue. But the epoch threading is critical \u2014 if a takeover lands between the outer WriterContext entry and the inbox-consume call, the threaded epoch is stale. The plan doesn't address this nested-staleness case.",

    "iteration": 4,
    "idea": "# Sprint 1 \u2014 Sessions and the binding contract\n\nThis is Sprint 1 of the Astrid reshape (full plan: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid/idea.md` \u2014 Sprint 1 section is canonical for scope; this brief expands it for execution).\n\nWorking directory: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/Astrid`. Branch: create `reshape/sprint-1` off `reshape/sprint-0` and work there.\n\n## Goal\n\nEnd multi-tab races. Make \"what am I bound to\" a first-class, queryable thing. Ship `astrid status` as the agent's mandated anchor verb. Lay down the lease + locked-append contract every later sprint depends on.\n\n**No timelines yet (Sprint 2). No new step model yet (Sprint 3).** Sprint 1 is purely the session/binding/safety layer.\n\n## Foundational invariant (the apex contract)\n\nThe single most important deliverable is the **locked event-append path**, because every later sprint runs on top of it. The contract is:\n\n> Every event appended to `runs/<ulid>/events.jsonl` does this **atomically inside one critical section** held by `flock(events.jsonl, LOCK_EX)`:\n> 1. Re-read the file's last line, parse its hash, compare against the writer's read-time hash. If they differ \u2192 reject with a stale-tail error.\n> 2. Re-read `runs/<ulid>/lease.json`'s `writer_epoch`. Compare against the writer's read-time epoch. If they differ \u2192 reject with a stale-epoch error.\n> 3. Compute the new event's previous-hash = the last-line hash.\n> 4. Append the new event line.\n> 5. fsync (or equivalent durability), then release the lock.\n\nA stale writer that passed its initial epoch check but lost the race to a takeover **gets rejected at append time, not silently committed**. The `flock` + last-hash CAS + epoch CAS are the actual fence; the takeover *event* is just observability.\n\nThis replaces the current `append_event` in `astrid/core/task/events.py`, which does read/verify/append **without a file lock** (the bug we're fixing).\n\nThe Sprint 0 flock-on-APFS spike confirmed `flock` is reliable for this workload. Use the worked test pattern from `tests/spikes/test_flock_apfs.py` as the integration-test starting point.\n\n## Decisions (locked; do not relitigate)\n\n- **Session id = ULID.** Stored in `~/.astrid/sessions/<ulid>.json` with `{session_id, project, timeline, run_id, agent_id, attached_at, last_used_at, role}`. `role` \u2208 `writer | reader`.\n- **`ASTRID_SESSION_ID` env var binds the tab.** Subprocesses inherit through `fork`/`exec`. Sprint 0 verified all existing subprocess paths preserve env via `{**os.environ, ...}`.\n- **Stable agent identity in `~/.astrid/identity.json`** with `agent_id: <slug>` (e.g. `claude-1`, `codex-research`). First-run bootstrap prompts user to set one. Sessions inherit it; per-tab override via `astrid attach --as agent:<id>`.\n- **Per-user default project in `~/.astrid/config.json`; per-workspace override in `.astrid/config.json`.** Neither auto-attaches \u2014 both feed the suggestion shown by `status` when unbound.\n- **Per-project default timeline pointer recorded in `project.json`** (Sprint 1 writes the field; Sprint 2 wires it into `astrid attach`). Use a sentinel value in S1's migration; Sprint 2 backfills.\n- **Read-only attach when a run is held by another session.** Second attacher gets `role: reader`. Takeover is the explicit verb `astrid sessions takeover <session_id>`.\n- **Run lease epoch (`writer_epoch: int`).** Stored in `runs/<ulid>/lease.json` alongside `attached_session.json` (the current writer's session id). Every mutating verb routes through the locked-append helper above.\n- **Takeover atomically increments `writer_epoch`** in the same locked write that swaps `attached_session.json`. Both old session and new session see a `takeover` event in `events.jsonl` with `{prev_session, new_session, prev_epoch, new_epoch, at}`.\n- **Stuck-attachment recovery via `status`.** A fresh tab's `status` proactively flags suspected-dead sessions (criterion: no event written in N seconds AND session-file mtime older than M seconds \u2014 pick honest defaults like 60s/300s) and offers takeover with a \"may still be live elsewhere \u2014 confirm\" warning. Manual `astrid sessions detach <id>` is the unconditional escape hatch.\n- **Threads die.** `thread show @active` and friends are removed. Any persistent thread state migrates into session bindings in a one-shot pass.\n- **Inbox primitive standardized.** Every run has `runs/<ulid>/inbox/`. Sessions and external systems write JSON files; `astrid next` consumes them. This is the substrate that makes async work possible from day one.\n- **First-run bootstrap is part of this sprint, not assumed.** Fresh tab post-migration must not produce a string of errors. When `~/.astrid/identity.json` is missing OR no default project is set, `astrid` prompts through identity + project discovery + default selection explicitly. `astrid status` when unbound + no default lists discoverable projects with the `attach` command spelled out (not just \"no session\").\n- **Migration sets a per-project default-timeline sentinel** even though Sprint 2 wires the actual timeline container. Otherwise Sprint 2 has to backfill across the entire project tree.\n- **CLI gate.** Every verb except `attach`, `status`, `projects ls`, `projects create`, `sessions ls`, `sessions takeover`, `init` (first-run bootstrap), and `--help` errors out with a clear \"no session bound\" message that suggests `astrid attach <project>`.\n\n## Deliverables\n\n### New / rewritten files\n\n- **`astrid/core/session/`** new package:\n  - `paths.py` \u2014 helpers for `~/.astrid/sessions/<ulid>.json`, `~/.astrid/identity.json`, `~/.astrid/config.json`, `.astrid/config.json` (workspace).\n  - `model.py` \u2014 `Session` dataclass (id, project, timeline, run_id, agent_id, attached_at, last_used_at, role) with json read/write.\n  - `binding.py` \u2014 resolve current session via `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` env var; readers and writers; \"is this session a writer for this run\" checks.\n  - `lease.py` \u2014 `runs/<ulid>/lease.json` reader/writer; `attached_session.json` reader/writer.\n  - `identity.py` \u2014 `~/.astrid/identity.json` reader/writer + first-run bootstrap.\n  - `config.py` \u2014 config file reader (user + workspace) + default project/timeline resolution.\n  - `discovery.py` \u2014 list discoverable projects under the projects root for the bootstrap UX.\n\n- **`astrid/core/task/events.py`** rewrite the append path:\n  - New `append_event_locked(run_dir, event_dict, *, expected_writer_epoch, expected_prev_hash) -> EventRecord` that does flock + last-hash CAS + epoch CAS atomically. Raises `StaleTailError` / `StaleEpochError` on conflict.\n  - Old `append_event` becomes a thin wrapper over the locked path that reads epoch + last-hash internally; deprecate it for callers that need the CAS guarantees.\n  - All existing call sites that mutate the event log MUST route through the new locked helper.\n\n- **`astrid/core/task/active_run.py`** delete or stub:\n  - The `<project>/active_run.json` file is the multi-tab race we're fixing. Migrate any existing files into per-tab session bindings (one-shot at `astrid attach`), then remove the read/write helpers. Leave a one-line stub explaining what replaced it.\n\n- **Threads subsystem removal:**\n  - Find every `thread show @active`-style verb and any `astrid/core/thread/` (or wherever threads live). Migrate persistent thread state into session bindings (best-effort one-shot migration script). Then remove the subsystem.\n\n### New / rewritten verbs\n\nUse the existing CLI module (`astrid/cli/` or wherever verbs are registered \u2014 discover by reading current code).\n\n- `astrid attach <project> [--timeline <slug>] [--session <id>] [--as agent:<id>]` \u2014 bind the current tab to a project. Creates a new session (writes `~/.astrid/sessions/<ulid>.json`), prints the `ASTRID_SESSION_ID=<ulid>` line for the user to `export`. If `--session <id>` given, resume an existing session. If a run is held by another writer, attach as reader and tell the user how to take over.\n- `astrid sessions ls` \u2014 list all sessions in `~/.astrid/sessions/` with their bound project + timeline + run + last-used.\n- `astrid sessions detach [<id>]` \u2014 explicit detach. With no id, detaches the current tab.\n- `astrid sessions takeover <run-id|session-id>` \u2014 atomic epoch-bump + lease swap. Emits a `takeover` event into the run's `events.jsonl`. Refuses if the session being taken over wrote within the last N seconds without confirmation flag.\n- `astrid status` \u2014 completely rewritten. When bound, prints the full breadcrumb: `session <id> \u00b7 agent <id> \u00b7 project <slug> \u00b7 timeline <slug> \u00b7 run <id> \u00b7 current step <name> \u00b7 recent events (last 5) \u00b7 inbox count \u00b7 role (writer/reader) \u00b7 takeover hint when reader`. When unbound: lists discoverable projects, suggests defaults if set, spells out the exact `astrid attach <slug>` command. When `~/.astrid/identity.json` missing: triggers the first-run bootstrap.\n- **CLI gate** on every other verb: read `ASTRID_SESSION_ID`, look up the session file, error-out with a clear hint if either is missing.\n\n### Migration scripts\n\nPlace under `scripts/migrations/sprint-1/`:\n\n- `migrate_active_run_to_sessions.py` \u2014 for each project with an `active_run.json`, materialize a session binding for \"the calling tab\" (best-effort: one session per project), then delete the file. Idempotent. Dry-run by default; `--apply` flag to commit.\n- `migrate_threads_to_sessions.py` \u2014 same shape for thread state, if it exists in the codebase.\n- `migrate_set_default_timeline_sentinel.py` \u2014 for each project, write a sentinel `default_timeline_id: null` field into `project.json` so Sprint 2 doesn't have to backfill.\n\nEach script:\n- Logs every action to stderr.\n- Supports `--dry-run` (default) and `--apply`.\n- Writes a per-project audit log to a temp file.\n\n### SKILL.md / AGENTS.md rewrite\n\nBoth files are stale (predate the task framework, don't teach `astrid status` / `next` / `ack`). Rewrite around the new mandated workflow:\n\n1. **First instruction**: \"Run `astrid status` before any other verb. If unbound, ask the user which project.\"\n2. Document `attach`, `status`, `next`, `ack`, `sessions ls/detach/takeover`.\n3. Stop-hook preamble (if Astrid has one \u2014 discover by reading current SKILL.md) is updated to re-inject the status-first instruction when context decays.\n\n### Tests\n\n- **Locked-append unit tests** (`tests/task/test_events_locked.py`): single-process correctness \u2014 append, append-after-stale-tail rejected, append-after-stale-epoch rejected, append after epoch bump succeeds.\n- **Two-tab integration tests** (use the existing `tests/concurrency/two_tab_harness.py` from Sprint 0): two writers race the same run; exactly one succeeds, the other gets stale-tail or stale-epoch. Run with `--count 100` to surface races.\n- **Takeover atomicity test**: takeover-during-append must either (a) the append wins and takeover sees stale tail and retries, or (b) the takeover wins and the appender sees stale epoch and rejects. Never both succeed; never silent loss.\n- **Session attach/detach/resume tests** (unit + small integration).\n- **Migration script tests**: each script has a smoke test with a tiny fixture project tree.\n- **First-run bootstrap test**: simulate missing `~/.astrid/identity.json` \u2192 bootstrap prompt path runs end-to-end (mock the prompt input).\n- **CLI gate test**: every gated verb errors clearly when `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` is unset.\n\n## Stop-lines (halt and rethink, do not push through)\n\n1. **If the locked-append path can't be proven correct under contention** (the two-tab harness shows interleaved writes or silent epoch-violations under any seed), halt. The whole reshape is built on this guarantee.\n2. **If session resume across tab restart is broken** (closing a tab + reopening + `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` re-export doesn't restore the binding), halt. The \"sessions are resumable, never auto-expire\" decision is load-bearing.\n3. **If migration of `active_run.json` causes ANY data loss** (any project's run-history becomes unreadable), halt and restore from the Sprint 0 snapshot.\n\n## Out of scope (Sprint 2 / 3 territory \u2014 do NOT touch)\n\n- Timeline as container (Sprint 2). The `default_timeline_id` field is a sentinel only; do not implement the timeline schema.\n- Step model rewrite (Sprint 3). `plan.py` / `inbox.py` / step adapter logic stays as-is; only the event-append path changes.\n- Plan mutation verbs (Sprint 3).\n- Any RunPod / vibecomfy work.\n- Hype port.\n- Auditing verbs (`astrid run show / artifacts / trace`) \u2014 those are Sprint 5a.\n\n## Acceptance criteria\n\n- `git branch --show-current` returns `reshape/sprint-1`.\n- All Sprint 0 tests still pass (regression).\n- `pytest tests/task/test_events_locked.py tests/concurrency/ tests/session/ -v` all green.\n- Two-tab harness: race the same `astrid attach` + locked-append-via-some-verb 100 times; exactly one writer succeeds per race, the other gets a clean stale-tail or stale-epoch error.\n- Fresh tab + no identity file \u2192 `astrid status` triggers first-run bootstrap, completes successfully, leaves `~/.astrid/identity.json` written.\n- Fresh tab + identity but no session bound \u2192 `astrid status` lists discoverable projects + spells out `astrid attach <slug>`.\n- Fresh tab + session bound \u2192 `astrid status` prints the full breadcrumb.\n- `astrid sessions takeover` of a stale-looking session works; the old session's next mutating verb gets rejected with stale-epoch.\n- All migration scripts run cleanly against the Sprint 0 baseline inventory (refer to `docs/reshape/inventory-baseline-20260511.csv`).\n- The Sprint 0 pinned regression workload (`docs/reshape/regression-workload.md`) re-runs cleanly. **Note:** that doc reports no past hype runs exist on this machine, so the regression gate is \"no functional regression\" rather than \"byte-equivalent output.\" Run any existing hype-related smoke tests and confirm green.\n- SKILL.md / AGENTS.md updated; a fresh-context agent reading them can complete one full task end-to-end without asking a clarifying question.\n\n## Useful repo references\n\n- `astrid/core/task/events.py` \u2014 the unsafe `append_event` to replace.\n- `astrid/core/task/active_run.py` \u2014 the file to migrate away from.\n- `astrid/core/project/paths.py` \u2014 project / run path helpers; reuse the patterns.\n- `tests/spikes/test_flock_apfs.py` \u2014 Sprint 0's flock spike; the worked pattern for the locked-append integration tests.\n- `tests/concurrency/two_tab_harness.py` \u2014 Sprint 0's race harness.\n- `docs/reshape/spike-env-inheritance.md` \u2014 confirms `ASTRID_SESSION_ID` survives every subprocess path.\n- `docs/reshape/spike-flock-apfs.md` \u2014 confirms `flock` is reliable for this workload.\n- `idea.md` \u00a7 Foundation > Load-bearing decisions \u2014 apex spec.\n- `idea.md` \u00a7 Sprint 1 \u2014 the canonical sprint definition this brief expands.\n\n## Style notes\n\n- Type hints everywhere. Pathlib over os.path.\n- New tests follow existing test naming + structure conventions in the repo.\n- Migration scripts: shebang, `chmod +x`, `--dry-run` is the default.\n- No silent fallbacks in the lease/append path \u2014 every CAS failure raises a typed exception with the conflicting values in the message.",

Replaces the deleted `<project>/active_run.json` with `<project>/current_run.json` (pure pointer, no plan_hash) plus `runs/<id>/lease.json` (writer fence) \u2014 preserves the \"one active run per project\" invariant (FLAG-002/003/correctness-1/correctness-2). Adds tail-seek 4KB read to `append_event_locked` (correctness-5), reuses existing `astrid/threads/ids.py:generate_ulid` via a thin re-export module instead of re-implementing (correctness-4), and makes `append_event` legacy wrapper explicitly test/migration-only with `WriterContext` mandated in production (correctness-3, criteria_quality-1). Adds Step 13 enumerating all 18 active_run test files plus a shared `attached_session` fixture (FLAG-009/scope-2). Specifies stop-hook session-precedence and drops the projects-root scan (all_locations-1); historical AGENT.md files left alone (all_locations-2); adds docs/HOOKS.md + docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md to the doc sweep (all_locations-3). Verb functions take a `session` argument so tests bypass the gate by design (callers-1); `gate_command` gains a `writer_epoch` kwarg so inbox re-entry threads the outer epoch through (callers-2); `astrid author test --project` bypasses the session gate as a developer/CI path (callers-3). Narrows CLI allowlist to brief-canonical set plus `sessions detach` (issue_hints-1/3); removes `doctor`/`setup` from allowlist. Variant-selection migration becomes a no-op stderr audit since threads stay in place (issue_hints-2). Tightens `default_timeline_id` validation to slug-typed (criteria_quality-2). Adds literal breadcrumb output template for status test (criteria_quality-3). Adds new test file `test_current_run.py` + new module `astrid/core/project/current_run.py`."

      {
        "id": "FLAG-002",
        "concern": "Binding-model gap: removing <project>/active_run.json without a project-level pointer to 'the current run' leaves cmd_attach <project> and the migration script unable to determine which runs/<ulid>/ is current when a project has multiple historical runs. Plan implies first-attach picks 'the unowned run' but defines neither the discovery rule nor a fallback when several runs are unowned.",
        "resolution": "v2 contracts the threads-removal scope (only the `astrid thread` user-facing CLI verb is removed; `astrid/threads/` stays as an internal library still used by orchestrator/executor runners and 5 pack run.py files \u2014 full removal deferred to a later sprint, resolving FLAG-001/008/scope-1/scope-3 and the hype-regression risk). Replaces the deleted `<project>/active_run.json` with `<project>/current_run.json` (pure pointer, no plan_hash) plus `runs/<id>/lease.json` (writer fence) \u2014 preserves the \"one active run per project\" invariant (FLAG-002/003/correctness-1/correctness-2). Adds tail-seek 4KB read to `append_event_locked` (correctness-5), reuses existing `astrid/threads/ids.py:generate_ulid` via a thin re-export module instead of re-implementing (correctness-4), and makes `append_event` legacy wrapper explicitly test/migration-only with `WriterContext` mandated in production (correctness-3, criteria_quality-1). Adds Step 13 enumerating all 18 active_run test files plus a shared `attached_session` fixture (FLAG-009/scope-2). Specifies stop-hook session-precedence and drops the projects-root scan (all_locations-1); historical AGENT.md files left alone (all_locations-2); adds docs/HOOKS.md + docs/orchestrator-v1-plan.md to the doc sweep (all_locations-3). Verb functions take a `session` argument so tests bypass the gate by design (callers-1); `gate_command` gains a `writer_epoch` kwarg so inbox re-entry threads the outer epoch through (callers-2); `astrid author test --project` bypasses the session gate as a developer/CI path (callers-3). Narrows CLI allowlist to brief-canonical set plus `sessions detach` (issue_hints-1/3); removes `doctor`/`setup` from allowlist. Variant-selection migration becomes a no-op stderr audit since threads stay in place (issue_hints-2). Tightens `default_timeline_id` validation to slug-typed (criteria_quality-2). Adds literal breadcrumb output template for status test (criteria_quality-3). Adds new test file `test_current_run.py` + new module `astrid/core/project/current_run.py`."
      },

      {
        "id": "FLAG-004",
        "concern": "ULID generator duplication: astrid/threads/ids.py already implements a 26-char Crockford ULID with monotonic-within-millisecond guarantees via counter state. Plan vendors a fresh 'time_ms + urandom(10)' generator that may regress on within-ms monotonicity unless counter state is added. Right move is to lift the existing module into astrid/core/session/ulid.py.",
        "resolution": "astrid/threads/ids.py:21-39 shows the existing implementation with _LAST_MS/_LAST_RANDOM counter state. Plan Phase 2 Step 5 describes a fresh implementation."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-006",
        "concern": "Inbox callers of gate_command: inbox.consume_inbox_entry (inbox.py:259) synthesizes an ack and re-invokes gate_command from inside `astrid next`. Plan does not specify whether the synthesized re-entry inherits the outer session's epoch/tail or re-reads them. Re-reading in a different lock acquisition can cause spurious StaleEpochError under takeover-during-consume.",
        "resolution": "inbox.py:259 calls gate_command(slug, synthesized, [], root=projects_root) from inside `astrid next`. Plan Phase 4 Step 10 migrates gate.py:519 to session-resolution but does not address inbox-consume re-entry."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-007",
        "concern": "append_event legacy wrapper retry semantics: wrapper reads epoch+tail outside the lock then calls append_event_locked. Under concurrent writes it raises StaleTailError to legacy callers; no retry. Either the wrapper needs a bounded retry loop or every legacy caller must migrate to WriterContext.",
        "resolution": "Plan Phase 1 Step 2 describes the wrapper but specifies no retry. Phase 4 Step 10 says all 20 sites migrate to WriterContext, making the wrapper essentially test-only."
      },
      {

      {

      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-010",
        "concern": "Plan-hash CAS migration is broken: v2 strips plan_hash from current_run.json and proposes 'tail-seek + parse run_started \u2192 cache' as the replacement for gate.py:528. But run_started is the FIRST event in events.jsonl; tail-seek of the last 4KB cannot find it on any non-trivial run. The CAS must either scan the whole file (defeating the perf claim) or plan_hash must be stored in lease.json or current_run.json. Real correctness gap introduced by v2.",
        "resolution": "v3 addresses the five concrete correctness gaps critique_v2 found, plus three smaller refinements, all within v2's design shape. (1) plan_hash moves into lease.json (resolves FLAG-010 / correctness-1 \u2014 v2's 'parse run_started from tail' was broken because run_started is the FIRST event, not the tail; reading plan_hash inside the same flock the append uses is free). (2) GateDecision gains writer_epoch + run_dir + session_id fields so the four post-dispatch record_* helpers (record_dispatch_complete at gate.py:1099, plus the three phase-5-reserved helpers at 1186/1201/1211) can each open their own short WriterContext via a new writer_context_from_decision() helper \u2014 resolves FLAG-011 / correctness-2 / callers-3 / criteria_quality. (3) WriterContext auto-rebinds stale session.run_id on entry by re-reading current_run.json and updating both the in-memory session and the on-disk session file \u2014 resolves correctness-3 (Tab A attaches with run_id=None, Tab B starts, Tab A's next verb rebinds without error). (4) Role enum gains 'orphan-pending' for the present-but-unclaimed lease case, with a clear takeover hint printed by cmd_attach \u2014 resolves correctness-4 ('writer without claim' was dishonest). (5) inbox.py:216 decision='abort' now calls BOTH clear_current_run AND release_writer_lease (full clear, matching existing clear_active_run semantic) \u2014 resolves correctness-5 behavior regression. Plus: tail-seek chunked follow-back doubles window up to file size, no 64KB cap (correctness-7); verify_chain at gate.py:533 replaced with tail-only verify done inside append_event_locked (correctness-6); cmd_next reorders so session check precedes preamble print (all_locations-3); SKILL.md line 62 retained with a clarifying note that pack-level '--thread' is a variant-lineage identifier unrelated to the removed CLI verb (scope, all_locations-1, issue_hints-2). callers-2 (nested staleness in inbox\u2192gate_command) is documented as correct behavior in the WriterContext contract: epoch is a fence, not a freshness guarantee. issue_hints-1 (doctor gating) confirmed acceptable \u2014 first-run bootstrap is handled by attach/init. issue_hints-3 already addressed in v2."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-011",
        "concern": "WriterContext coverage gap: gate.py:1105 (record_dispatch_complete), 1186-1198 (record_step_attested), 1201-1208 (record_nested_entered), 1211-1218 (record_nested_exited) all call append_event from OUTSIDE gate_command. record_dispatch_complete is invoked from pipeline.main()'s `finally:` block at line 59, after _dispatch returns. _run_inline_checks (gate.py:1136, 1146, 1155, 1164) is called inside _dispatch_code/_dispatch_attested so it IS within gate_command \u2014 OK. But the four record_* helpers are out-of-band. Plan v2's 'wrap gate_command body in WriterContext' strategy does not reach them. The must-criterion 'every site in gate.py uses WriterContext' will fail unless GateDecision carries the writer epoch into the post-dispatch path or each record_* helper opens its own WriterContext from session resolution.",
        "resolution": "v3 addresses the five concrete correctness gaps critique_v2 found, plus three smaller refinements, all within v2's design shape. (1) plan_hash moves into lease.json (resolves FLAG-010 / correctness-1 \u2014 v2's 'parse run_started from tail' was broken because run_started is the FIRST event, not the tail; reading plan_hash inside the same flock the append uses is free). (2) GateDecision gains writer_epoch + run_dir + session_id fields so the four post-dispatch record_* helpers (record_dispatch_complete at gate.py:1099, plus the three phase-5-reserved helpers at 1186/1201/1211) can each open their own short WriterContext via a new writer_context_from_decision() helper \u2014 resolves FLAG-011 / correctness-2 / callers-3 / criteria_quality. (3) WriterContext auto-rebinds stale session.run_id on entry by re-reading current_run.json and updating both the in-memory session and the on-disk session file \u2014 resolves correctness-3 (Tab A attaches with run_id=None, Tab B starts, Tab A's next verb rebinds without error). (4) Role enum gains 'orphan-pending' for the present-but-unclaimed lease case, with a clear takeover hint printed by cmd_attach \u2014 resolves correctness-4 ('writer without claim' was dishonest). (5) inbox.py:216 decision='abort' now calls BOTH clear_current_run AND release_writer_lease (full clear, matching existing clear_active_run semantic) \u2014 resolves correctness-5 behavior regression. Plus: tail-seek chunked follow-back doubles window up to file size, no 64KB cap (correctness-7); verify_chain at gate.py:533 replaced with tail-only verify done inside append_event_locked (correctness-6); cmd_next reorders so session check precedes preamble print (all_locations-3); SKILL.md line 62 retained with a clarifying note that pack-level '--thread' is a variant-lineage identifier unrelated to the removed CLI verb (scope, all_locations-1, issue_hints-2). callers-2 (nested staleness in inbox\u2192gate_command) is documented as correct behavior in the WriterContext contract: epoch is a fence, not a freshness guarantee. issue_hints-1 (doctor gating) confirmed acceptable \u2014 first-run bootstrap is handled by attach/init. issue_hints-3 already addressed in v2."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-012",
        "concern": "Session.run_id drift vs current_run.json: cmd_attach with no current_run.json sets session.run_id=None. If another tab subsequently runs cmd_start, current_run.json is written but the first tab's session file is NOT updated. The first tab now has stale session.run_id=None while the project has an active run. Plan does not specify whether the first tab's verbs auto-rebind (re-read current_run.json) or error. Same multi-tab-state-divergence class of bug the sprint exists to fix.",
        "resolution": "Plan step 10 cmd_attach: 'if absent \u2192 run_id=None, role=writer (no run yet, ready for cmd_start).' Step 12 cmd_start: 'replace write_active_run(slug, run_id, plan_hash, \u2026) with two calls: write_current_run(slug, run_id) AND lease.write_lease_init(run_dir, session.id). Session's run_id is set + session file rewritten' \u2014 but only THIS session's file, not the other tab's."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-013",
        "concern": "cmd_attach 'present and unowned \u2192 role=writer, do NOT yet acquire the lease' creates a dead-end state: cmd_start refuses (current_run.json present), and only `astrid sessions takeover` can claim the orphan lease. Calling this state role=writer is misleading because the session cannot mutate. Either attach should auto-claim under the lease lock (incrementing epoch as a takeover would), or rename to role=orphan-pending so callers know they need explicit takeover.",
        "resolution": "Plan step 10 cmd_attach discovery rule: 'if present and unowned \u2192 role=writer, do NOT yet acquire the lease (lease is acquired by astrid start or by an explicit takeover).' Step 12 cmd_start guard rejects if current_run.json exists, so this writer can never start."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-014",
        "concern": "Inbox-abort semantic regression: current inbox.py:212-217 calls clear_active_run(slug, ...) on decision='abort' (full clear). Plan v2 step 12 inbox.py:216 bullet says only 'release_writer_lease(run_dir)' and explicitly 'clear_current_run is NOT called here.' But the abort decision should logically clear current_run.json too (the run IS aborted). Otherwise current_run.json points to an aborted run and `cmd_start` is blocked until manual cleanup. Likely a plan-author misread of existing semantics.",
        "resolution": "Existing inbox.py:212-217 calls clear_active_run for decision='abort'. Plan v2 step 12 inbox.py:216 bullet: 'inbox.py:216 (clear on consume): release_writer_lease(run_dir) (run continues but writer slot is freed). clear_current_run is NOT called here \u2014 consume-then-abort vs consume-then-continue is the existing semantic.'"
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-015",
        "concern": "verify_chain still called at gate.py:533 on every gate_command entry. Plan v2 retires verify_chain from the append path but leaves it in the gate path. Combined with the gate's 4KB tail-seek append, verify_chain becomes the bottleneck for any long-running plan (full O(n) chain re-validation on each verb invocation). Plan should either keep this site (acknowledging cost is per-verb not per-append) or migrate it to a lazy 'last-K events' check.",
        "resolution": "gate.py:533: `ok, _last_index, error = verify_chain(events_path)`. Plan step 2 says 'Drop verify_chain from this path (kept as audit-only)' for the append helper but does not address the gate-entry site."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-016",
        "concern": "Tail-seek 64KB cap: plan v2 specifies '4KB window, expand to 64KB then fail loudly' for the seek-from-end tail read. Events that include user feedback or evidence strings could plausibly exceed 64KB (free-form text). Either expand the fallback to follow the file in larger chunks or document a hard limit on event size enforced at append time.",
        "resolution": "Plan v2 Step 2 bullet 'tail-seek read': 'If the 4KB window has no newline (single oversized line), expand to 64KB then fail loudly.' Events can carry `feedback` strings (lifecycle_ack.py:write_iteration_feedback)."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-018",
        "concern": "SKILL.md line 62 still documents `iteration_video.run inspect <thread>`, and iteration_video/run.py:40 still references `--thread @active`. Plan v2 removes only line 60 but leaves the iteration_video thread documentation intact. The plan's claim 'Remove every thread / @active / variant-selection / prefix-attribute reference from agent-facing prose' is therefore only partially achievable while threads/ remains in production use. Worth documenting that the iteration_video thread argument is a pack-internal concept that agents may still encounter.",
        "resolution": "SKILL.md:62 references `inspect <thread>` argument. iteration_video/run.py:40 reads `thread_ref` defaulting to '@active'. Plan step 18 only describes removing the line-60 paragraph."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-019",
        "concern": "Tail-only verify formula is incorrect: plan v3 step 2 reads 'recompute _event_hash(parsed_tail.prev_hash, parsed_tail) and compare with parsed_tail.hash.' But events.py stores only `hash` on each event \u2014 prev_hash is not a stored field; it's a parameter to _event_hash. To recompute the tail's hash you must read the second-to-last line's `hash` value (= prev_hash for the tail). As written, `parsed_tail.prev_hash` is None/AttributeError. Either drop tail-only verify (the CAS already guarantees integrity for the current append) or extend the tail-seek to also parse the second-to-last line.",
        "resolution": "v4 lands six concrete code-level fixes to v3 plus three smaller refinements: (1) DROP the broken tail-only chain verify formula (FLAG-019/correctness-1) \u2014 events.py doesn't store prev_hash; the StaleTailError CAS on tail_hash == expected_prev_hash is already sufficient integrity for the current append. Full-chain verify_chain stays callable for tests + audit; a future `astrid audit verify-chain` verb is deferred to Sprint 5a. (2) ADD writer-authorization check to WriterContext.__enter__ via new NotWriterError (FLAG-020/correctness-2/callers/criteria_quality-1) \u2014 `lease['attached_session_id'] != self.session.id` raises; readers and orphan-pending sessions cannot mutate. (3) DOCUMENT cmd_start write ordering: lease.json FIRST, then current_run.json (correctness-4/all_locations-2) \u2014 ensures auto-rebind readers never observe a current_run.json without a corresponding lease. Document in current_run.py and in Step 12 with explicit code comment. (4) SD-023 amendment (correctness-5/criteria_quality-2): preamble precedes operator-facing messages WITHIN a bound session; the 'no session bound' error is the explicit exception. Amend preamble.py top-of-file comment and lifecycle.py:407-411 comment. (5) Migration STOP-LINE on malformed active_run.json (scope-2): hard abort by default; opt-in `--force-skip-malformed` for explicit skip. (6) Auto-rebind side-effect on session file documented in WriterContext docstring AND in the `attached_session` fixture, which provides a `refresh()` helper (all_locations-1). Plus: discoverable TODO(sprint-N) marker in astrid/threads/__init__.py (scope-1); new writer-auth tests for reader and orphan-pending refusal (Step 14); new mid-chain corruption visibility tests (Step 3 + Step 19) confirm verify_chain still catches it offline (correctness-3). issue_hints/issue_hints-1 confirmed acceptable trade-offs."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-020",
        "concern": "WriterContext lacks writer-authorization check: plan v3 Step 9 captures lease['writer_epoch'] and lease['plan_hash'] but never verifies `lease['attached_session_id'] == self.session.id`. A reader or orphan-pending session can enter WriterContext, see the matching (unchanged) epoch, and successfully append events. The epoch CAS only catches takeovers (which bump epoch); it does NOT catch an unauthorized session that holds a stale role. Plan should add `if lease['attached_session_id'] != self.session.id: raise NotWriterError(...)` at __enter__.",
        "resolution": "v4 lands six concrete code-level fixes to v3 plus three smaller refinements: (1) DROP the broken tail-only chain verify formula (FLAG-019/correctness-1) \u2014 events.py doesn't store prev_hash; the StaleTailError CAS on tail_hash == expected_prev_hash is already sufficient integrity for the current append. Full-chain verify_chain stays callable for tests + audit; a future `astrid audit verify-chain` verb is deferred to Sprint 5a. (2) ADD writer-authorization check to WriterContext.__enter__ via new NotWriterError (FLAG-020/correctness-2/callers/criteria_quality-1) \u2014 `lease['attached_session_id'] != self.session.id` raises; readers and orphan-pending sessions cannot mutate. (3) DOCUMENT cmd_start write ordering: lease.json FIRST, then current_run.json (correctness-4/all_locations-2) \u2014 ensures auto-rebind readers never observe a current_run.json without a corresponding lease. Document in current_run.py and in Step 12 with explicit code comment. (4) SD-023 amendment (correctness-5/criteria_quality-2): preamble precedes operator-facing messages WITHIN a bound session; the 'no session bound' error is the explicit exception. Amend preamble.py top-of-file comment and lifecycle.py:407-411 comment. (5) Migration STOP-LINE on malformed active_run.json (scope-2): hard abort by default; opt-in `--force-skip-malformed` for explicit skip. (6) Auto-rebind side-effect on session file documented in WriterContext docstring AND in the `attached_session` fixture, which provides a `refresh()` helper (all_locations-1). Plus: discoverable TODO(sprint-N) marker in astrid/threads/__init__.py (scope-1); new writer-auth tests for reader and orphan-pending refusal (Step 14); new mid-chain corruption visibility tests (Step 3 + Step 19) confirm verify_chain still catches it offline (correctness-3). issue_hints/issue_hints-1 confirmed acceptable trade-offs."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-021",
        "concern": "cmd_start write ordering for current_run.json + lease.json visibility: plan v3 says cmd_start writes both files, but doesn't specify ordering. WriterContext auto-rebind reads current_run.json then lease.json outside any lock. If a tab observes current_run.json after Tab B's write but before lease.json is flushed, lease.json may not exist yet. Plan should specify: cmd_start writes lease.json FIRST (atomic), then current_run.json (atomic), so any reader that sees the new current_run.json is guaranteed to find the corresponding lease.json.",
        "resolution": "Plan v3 Step 12 lifecycle.py:201 bullet: 'write_current_run(slug, run_id) AND write_lease_init(...)' \u2014 ordering unspecified."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-022",
        "concern": "Tail-only verify is weaker than verify_chain: if events.jsonl is corrupted in the middle (e.g., disk error wrote zeros into line 50 of a 200-event log), tail-only verify at gate-entry won't detect it. Plan v3 says full-chain verify becomes an audit verb but does not list an acceptance criterion that anything other than the audit verb (which a user must explicitly invoke) catches mid-chain corruption. Document the trade-off or add a periodic full-verify.",
        "resolution": "Plan v3 Phase 1 Step 2: 'replaces verify_chain on the hot path AND for gate.py:533. Full-chain verify becomes an explicit audit verb.' No criterion for when full-chain verify is actually run."
      },

        Requirements:
- Implement the intent, not just the text.
- Adapt if repository reality contradicts the plan.
- Report deviations explicitly.

- Do NOT fix unrelated issues you encounter (e.g., dependency compatibility, Python version workarounds). Only change files directly needed for the task. If tests need updating, only update tests that are directly related to your fix.
- If you cannot build the project from source (e.g., C extension compilation failures), report the build failure explicitly. Do NOT fall back to testing against an installed or cached package — that tests the wrong codebase and produces false positives.
- If you cannot verify your changes (tests missing or unrunnable), treat this as high risk — re-examine your implementation with extra scrutiny instead of accepting it on faith.
- If tests fail, read the traceback carefully. Diagnose WHY — don't just retry. Common causes: wrong function/method used, missing import, incorrect type, edge case not handled. Fix the root cause, then re-run.
- When verifying changes, run the entire test file or module (e.g., `pytest tests/test_foo.py`), not individual test functions. Individual tests miss regressions in the same module.
- finalize.json includes baseline_test_failures — a list of test IDs that were already failing before your changes. If a test fails and its ID appears in baseline_test_failures, it is pre-existing — do not scope-creep into fixing it. If baseline_test_failures is null, the baseline could not be captured; use your judgment but err on the side of assuming failures are regressions. You MUST still re-run the FULL test suite with your changes applied — pre-existing failures do not excuse skipping verification. Never narrow to individual test functions and stop.
- Before declaring the work complete, write a short script (not a full test) that reproduces the exact bug or incorrect behavior described in the task. Run it to confirm the fix resolves the issue. Then delete the script so it does not appear in the final diff. If the task description is too vague to write a concrete reproduction, note this explicitly in executor_notes.
- Output concrete files changed and commands run. `files_changed` means files you WROTE or MODIFIED — not files you read or verified. Only list files where you made actual edits.
- Use the tasks in `finalize.json` as the execution boundary.
